❯ would it be faster if we skip tests                                           

⏺ No. Tests aren't what's slow — three serial agent handoffs per rule are, and
  the tests are a small slice of one of them.

  1. What the time actually goes on. Each rule spins up a dev agent, then a
     review agent, then a land agent, each with its own context to build up
     before it does anything. Writing a spec is maybe a quarter of the dev
     agent's work. Running it is seconds.
  2. Skipping tests costs more than it saves. The review agent's verdict
     currently rests on something objective — the spec asserts the proposed
     value, not just that the rule fired. Take that away and review becomes
     "read it and reason about it", which is slower per rule and wrong more
     often. A rule that fails review costs a full extra dev+review cycle, which
     dwarfs what the spec saved.
  3. The real lever is batching. One dev agent doing P25–P32 in a single
     context, then one review, then one commit per batch. That kills most of the
     per-rule spin-up. It weakens your resume guarantee from per-rule to
     per-batch, which is still fine for stopping mid-run. [DECISION] — batch
     size is yours; 5–8 keeps a lost batch cheap.
  4. Cheaper cut if you want one. just rules-sync re-runs nest build after the
     dev and review agents have each already built. Three full Nest builds per
     rule. That's dead weight the tests aren't.

✻ Brewed for 26s · done Sunday 11:38 PM

❯ its also tokens, too many are being spent .                                   

⏺ The tokens go on re-reading, not on writing. Three fresh agents per rule × 122
  rules is 366 contexts, and each one boots by reading the same 122-row
  catalogue, the same requirements file, the same deferred list, and an existing
  rule as an example — to produce about fifteen lines of code.

  1. Inline the catalogue row. The workflow already knows which rule id it's
     building. Pulling that one table row out of the markdown and putting it in
     the prompt means no agent ever opens the 122-row file. That alone removes
     the single largest repeated read, three times per rule.
  2. Batch. Same fix as for speed, bigger effect on tokens: one dev context
     covering eight rules pays the preamble once instead of eight times, and it
     already has the example rule in context after the first one.
  3. Stop the review agent re-running the build. It runs just build and just 
     test immediately after the dev agent did, and reads both outputs in full.
     The test output grows every rule. Having it read only the diff and the test
     result summary keeps the independence without paying for the transcript
     twice.
  4. Model. Dev and review are running on Opus for mechanical work — a table row
     in, a small pure function and a spec out. Sonnet handles this shape, and
     the review agent's job is comparison against an entry that's now inline in
     its prompt.

  [DECISION] 1 and 3 are safe to do mid-run. 2 and 4 change the shape of what's
  already in flight, so they'd want the current P16–P40 run finished or stopped
  first.

  Searched for 1 pattern, ran 3 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Workflow(dynamic workflow: rules)
  ⎿  /workflows to view dynamic workflow runs

⏺ Running from P31 on the reshaped workflow — 92 rules in 16 batches.

  Four things changed, and the largest is the one you'd least expect:

  1. The rules landed so far average ~500 lines each for about 40 lines of 
     logic. P30 is a 200-line doc comment enumerating every case the code
     already states. That output was the biggest single spend, and every later
     agent then paid to read one as its example. There's now a 15-line comment
     budget and an 80-line spec budget, with the comment still required to state
     what it catches, what it proposes, why the ambiguity flag, the boundary
     against neighbouring rules, and why an approved row stops matching — once
     each.
  2. No agent opens the notes any more. The six binding requirements are written
     into the prompt, a complete example rule is inline, and a grep pulls only
     the batch's table rows plus the headings that place them. That removes
     three large reads per rule.
  3. Six rules per dev context, one review per batch reading the diff rather
     than re-running the build, one rules-sync per batch instead of per rule.
     Commits stay per-rule, so history and resume are unchanged — the resume
     unit is now the batch.
  4. All three stages on Sonnet.

  Two things worth knowing: P01–P30 keep their long comments, so the repo will
  read inconsistently unless you want them trimmed later. And P41–P48 turned out
  not to be blocked — nothing in the weight rules spans two columns; the
  pounds→kg conversion that does isn't in the catalogue at all.

       8  [`legacy_export/EXPORT-NOTES.md`](legacy_export/EXPORT-NOTES.md

       9  
      10  All patient data in this repository is synthetic. No real perso
          n appears in it.
      11 +                                                               
      12 +## Running it                                                  
      13 +                                                               
      14 +Needs Node 22 (see `.nvmrc`) and [`just`](https://github.com/ca
         +sey/just).                                                     
      15 +                                                               
      16 +```sh                                                          
      17 +nvm use                                                        
      18 +just install                                                   
      19 +cp .env.example .env                                           
      20 +```                                                            
      21 +                                                               
      22 +Then load the legacy export and register the rules:            
      23 +                                                               
      24 +```sh                                                          
      25 +just import                                                    
      26 +just rules-sync                                                
      27 +```                                                            
      28 +                                                               
      29 +`just import` creates `data/dev.sqlite`, creates the schema in 
         +it, and reads                                                  
      30 +`legacy_export/` into the legacy tables. There is no migration 
         +step — the schema                                              
      31 +has one final state, so TypeORM builds it on connect. Re-runnin
         +g the import                                                   
      32 +skips ids that are already there, so it is safe to repeat.     
      33 +                                                               
      34 +`just rules-sync` reconciles the rule catalogue in code into th
         +e `rule` and                                                   
      35 +`rule_version` tables. It reads code and writes the database, n
         +ever the other                                                 
      36 +way round, and it is idempotent — run it after adding a rule, a
         +nd again after an                                              
      37 +interrupted run.                                               
      38 +                                                               
      39 +```sh                                                          
      40 +just dev                                                       
      41 +```                                                            
      42 +                                                               
      43 +The API listens on the `PORT` in `.env` (3000 by default) and t
         +he web app on                                                  
      44 +<http://localhost:5173>. If something else on your machine alre
         +ady holds 3000,                                                
      45 +change `PORT` and `VITE_API_BASE_URL` together — the frontend r
         +eads the second                                                
      46 +to find the first.                                             
      47 +                                                               
      48 +### The rest of the commands                                   
      49 +                                                               
      50 +| | |                                                          
      51 +|---|---|                                                      
      52 +| `just test` | the backend test suite (Vitest). The web app ha
         +s no tests, by decision |                                      
      53 +| `just build` | build both workspaces |                       
      54 +| `just lint` / `just format` | ESLint and Prettier across the 
         +repository |                                                   
      55 +| `just import <dir>` | import an export other than `legacy_exp
         +ort/` |                                                        
      56 +| `just revise queue` | every rule version waiting to be revise
         +d |                                                            
      57 +| `just revise apply <file.json>` | apply one revision |       
      58 +                                                               
      59 +A bare `just` lists them.                                      
      60 +                                                               
      61 +### Pointing at Turso                                          
      62 +                                                               
      63 +Set `DATABASE_URL` to the `libsql://` URL and add `TURSO_AUTH_T
         +OKEN`. Nothing                                                 
      64 +else changes: the scheme of that one variable selects the drive
         +r, so there is no                                              
      65 +environment flag and no second set of connection settings.

      166 +          </Text>                                             
      167 +        </Table.Caption>                                      
      168        </Table>
      169      </Table.ScrollContainer>
      170    );

  Searched for 1 pattern, ran 5 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Done — the before/after cells are now a real character-level diff.

  Red for what the column holds, green for what's proposed, with the run that
  actually changed highlighted inside each, matched by longest common
  subsequence so "  P-0310  " → "P-0310" shows four red spaces rather than
  painting the whole value.

  Invisible characters get drawn: a space as · where it changed or where it pads
  the ends, tab and newline their own marks, and anything else — non-breaking
  space, zero-width space, control characters — as ⟨U+00A0⟩. Ordinary spaces
  inside a word stay as spaces, so a full name still reads as a name. A caption
  names the glyphs once per table.

  Two things:

  1. Not visually verified. It type-checks and builds, but I haven't seen it
     rendered — nothing is serving, and starting the API would have it writing
     dist/ while the running rules workflow builds into the same place. Worth a
     look once the run finishes.
  2. Ambiguous rules show their before value plainly, not in red. They propose
     nothing, so nothing is being removed — but the invisible characters still
     get drawn, which is often the whole of what's wrong with the cell.

  One correction to what I told you earlier: the servers were running when I
  said nothing was serving. My check from Bash couldn't reach localhost, so I
  read a blocked connection as a dead server rather than verifying it another
  way.

  Two consequences:

  1. They're now running under me, not your terminal. Output goes to a task log
     instead of your shell. Say the word if you want them back in your own
     terminal instead.
  2. The API writes dist/ while the rules workflow also builds there. nest start
     --watch and the workflow's just rules-sync share apps/api/dist. If a rule
     lands mid-watch you may see a spurious rebuild or a transient crash in the
     API — restart it if that happens; it won't corrupt anything.

✻ Sautéed for 47s · done Monday 10:49 AM

❯ of 84 rules only 1 is really matching ?                                       

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
  ⎿  Interrupted · What should Claude do instead?

❯ ambiguos rule also have approve decline ? what will approve do                

  Searched for 3 patterns, listed 1 directory, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Yes, it shows both — and the tick on an ambiguous row can only fail. That's a
  bug.

  Approve would try to write the proposed value into the column, but an
  ambiguous rule proposes nothing. The backend catches it and refuses with "it
  proposes no value", so no data is touched; the press just errors. Nothing is
  erased — the guard is explicit about why, since there's no undo for an
  accepted change.

  Decline on those rows is meaningful: it's how you say this row isn't a
  problem, and it's remembered for every future version of that rule. So is the
  revise checkbox — for an ambiguous rule that's the whole point of the screen,
  since there's nothing to accept.

  [DECISION] Two ways to fix the tick: hide it on ambiguous rules entirely, or
  keep it and have it mean "I've looked at this, stop showing it to me" — which
  is what decline already does. Hiding it is the honest one; the screen then
  offers exactly the two actions that exist.

  Ran 3 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Ambiguous rows now ask you for the answer instead of offering a tick that can
  only fail.

  Each one carries a box prefilled with what the column holds now, plus Apply —
  what you type becomes that row's proposal and goes down the existing path:
  same transaction, same write, same modification log. Blank is a real answer,
  meaning the column should hold nothing. Below it, a reason box and Decline,
  since on a row nobody can propose a value for, why you waved it through is the
  only thing left to record. And a quiet "The rule itself is wrong" that still
  opens the dialog, which is where the tick that parks the whole version lives.

  The panel's one-line note is now an alert that says what the rule can and
  can't do and which of the three presses to use.

  Verified against the live API: typing willem.ricci@icloud.com over
  willem.ricci @icloud.com on a P12 row wrote it to the patient and marked the
  finding approved with that value; declining another P12 row stored the reason.
  Supplying a value for a rule that already proposes one is refused with
  nothing written.

  One deliberate limit: you can only supply a value where the rule proposes
  none. Overriding a rule's own proposal would make the finding a record of
  something the rule never said — that's the cross, or a new version.

⏺ Done. Mantine has no detail-row component, but Table.Td passes colSpan through
  to the <td>, which is all it needed — the finding now spans every column
  directly under its row, with no top border or top padding so the two read as
  one entry. Striping is off for ambiguous rules so the zebra doesn't shade a
  row and its finding differently. Before gets the width back.

  One thing you'll see immediately: that text is the rule's description, so it's
  identical under every row — the same sentence repeated 21 times down a P12
  listing, and it's already in the alert at the top of the panel. The per-row
  copy is what 1.2.3 asks for, but if it reads as noise, dropping it costs
  nothing now that the alert says the same thing once.

⏺ Three things, and only one is real work.

  1. The multi-column fix (1.1.4) is specified but not built. The requirement
     says a fix may span several columns of one row — shown together, approved
     together, declined together — and the code still addresses findings one
     column at a time. Row approve, row decline and the detail table all act per
     column, so the screen can currently apply half a fix. No rule in the
     catalogue emits two columns for one row yet, so nothing is broken today;
     the gap is real the moment one does, and the pounds→kg rule the requirement
     uses as its own example is exactly that rule.
  2. 14 rules left in the catalogue — C06–C13 and D01–D06. P01–P65, I01–I38 and
     C01–C05 are landed. The workflow that was building them is no longer
     running, so that needs relaunching.
  3. Promotion (1.4) is deferred by the requirement itself — the main table
     doesn't exist and the text says it's next phase, including whether Part B
     intake writes into the same schema. Not a gap.

  The modification log (1.3) needs nothing: it says the rule row is the log,
  written by the same transaction as the data change, which is what 1.2.5
  already does. The revision workflow (1.5) exists as revise.js with just revise
  queue / apply behind it.

❯ 1. this is needed for us for specific rules , anything stopping us from doing 
  it ?                                                                          
  2. we need to have a main table with all rows and their status . with         
  filtering . have better names "Import clean" , "Import rejected" , "Import    
  pending"                                                                      
     clicking them show show the rules and have a approve all decline all       
  button which will do bulk update for this row . is that clean ?               
     we can also have a edit button that shows a form to update any field. and  
  also provide option to approve/decline import.                                

⏺ 1. Nothing blocks it. The contract already carries it — a rule returns
  updates[] with a column per entry, and two updates for one row land as two
  finding rows. And they're already grouped correctly without any schema change:
  a rule is atomic, so every finding for one (legacyId, ruleId, version) is one
  fix by definition. The work is widening the approve and decline where-clauses
  to drop column from the address, and grouping the detail table by legacy id
  so one entry shows N column diffs under one tick and one cross. The column
  becomes redundant in a row address, which makes the API smaller, not bigger.
  It touches the two controllers, their services, the client and the table, plus
  the existing tests that assert column-addressed behaviour.

  2. The row screen is sound. Three things in it need deciding.

  1. Only "rejected" is a status; "clean" and "pending" are derived. A row with
     no pending findings is clean — computing that from the findings you already
     have keeps one source of truth, whereas a stored clean flag goes stale the
     next time Apply runs and finds something new. Rejection is an operator
     decision and nothing can derive it, so that one gets a column. One nullable
     decision field, two computed states.
  2. The edit form is the only part that breaks something. Every change to
     legacy data currently goes through a rule row, and that row is the
     modification log (1.3) — which rule, which version, which column, from what
     to what. A free-form edit has no rule behind it, so it either punches a
     hole in the log or changes its shape. The clean way out is the one we just
     used for ambiguous rows: record a hand edit as a finding under a reserved
     rule id, so the log stays one shape and still answers "why did this value
     change". [DECISION] — that's a real design call, not a detail.
  3. Approve-all will trip on ambiguous findings. They propose nothing, so a
     bulk approve over a row hits exactly the failure the tick had — and most
     rows have at least one. It has to skip them and report how many it left, or
     the button fails on nearly every row.

  One more: 1.4 deferred this table partly on whether Part B intake writes into 
  the same tables. If it does, that schema wants designing once, now — not
  twice.

✻ Worked for 43s · done Monday 11:47 AM

❯ 2-> yes lets keep it after we have imported them.                             
  3 -> approve all , approves all rules for that row alone . its same as going  
  to each rule , finding that row and manually clicking aprpove.                

⏺ Both settled, and one consequence falls out.

  Approve-all being exactly "every row-approve you could have clicked" resolves
  the ambiguous problem rather than dodging it: those findings have no approve
  button to click any more — they have a value box — so they're simply not in
  the set. The button does the non-ambiguous pending findings for that row, in
  one transaction, and says how many it left.

  The consequence lands on the status model: a row with any ambiguous finding 
  can never reach "Import clean" by approve-all. Someone has to type a value or
  decline each one. On this dataset that's most rows — P12 emails, P18 dates,
  the whole ambiguous half of the catalogue — so "Import pending" will be the
  large bucket for a while, and the row screen's real job is getting those
  answered, not clicking approve-all.

  Recording a hand edit as a finding under a reserved rule id keeps the log one
  shape, so the modification log still answers "why did this value change" for
  every change, including the ones a human made.

  Meanwhile I've relaunched the rule workflow for the last 14 — C06–C13 and
  D01–D06.

⏺ Written into notes/final-requirements.md as 1.6 The rows screen (seven
  scenarios: the list and its filter, which states are derived vs stored,
  expanding a row, approve all, decline all, rejection, hand edit) plus 1.2.13
  for the ambiguous-finding box, which we built two commits ago and never
  specified.

  Two things I decided while writing, both worth your eye before I build on
  them:

  1. Rejection is reversible, unlike an accepted change. Rejecting writes
     nothing to the data, so taking it back costs nothing — which makes it a
     different kind of decision from 1.2.11's finality.
  2. Decline all covers ambiguous findings, approve all doesn't. The cross needs
     no proposal to press; the tick does. And decline all declines rows only —
     the ticked cross that parks a whole version is a judgement about a rule,
     which has no meaning applied to everything on one patient at once.

  I also made 1.6 say explicitly that it isn't 1.4, since "main table" was doing
  double duty — the table rows get promoted into is still next phase and still
  undesigned.

⏺ The rows screen is built — all five tasks landed on the first review pass,
  tree clean, 750 tests green.

  Two things worth your attention:

  1. Test files are type-checked by nothing. nest build only covers what
     tsconfig.build.json includes, which excludes test/, and vitest transforms
     with SWC, which type-checks nothing. A duplicate column key in the new seed
     helper went through both unnoticed — harmless here (the dead default was
     overridden), but the gap is real and will hide a worse one. A tsc --noEmit
     over the full config would catch it; it currently reports two pre-existing
     errors from the contract specs importing the web client across workspace
     boundaries, so closing the gap means fixing those first.
  2. The list is paged at 50, fixed server-side. The planning agent chose paging
     over a cap so every one of the 2466 rows stays reachable, and fixed the
     size because there's no virtualisation package in the stack. Worth knowing
     before you use the screen — filtering to "Import pending" will be a lot of
     pages.

  The servers are still running from earlier, so it should be live at
  localhost:5173 once Vite picks up the new route.

  Ran 3 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Both done. 748 tests green, both apps build.

  Approve on ambiguous — the per-finding tick was already gone; what you were
  seeing is "Approve rule" at the bottom of an ambiguous rule's panel. Ambiguity
  is rule-wide, so every pending row proposes nothing and that press could only
  500 with nothing written. I fixed the row tick earlier and missed its bigger
  sibling. It's now removed rather than disabled — there's no state of an
  ambiguous rule in which it becomes pressable. "Approve all" on the rows screen
  stays but is disabled when none of a row's pending findings proposes a value,
  since a row can mix both kinds.

  Virtual list — Mantine core has no virtualiser, so @tanstack/react-virtual:
  headless, ~5kB, no opinion about your markup, which matters because this list
  is an accordion rather than a table. GET /rows now returns the whole filtered
  set and the page parameter is gone; the screen draws only what's on screen
  inside a 640px scroll container. It measures each item as it's drawn rather
  than trusting the estimate — an expanded row is many times a collapsed one's
  height, and expanding must not shove everything below it out of place.

  One consequence: the list no longer scrolls with the document, it scrolls
  inside its own box. If that reads wrong once you look at it, the height is one
  constant.

  response is still the wrong shape as the dataset grows, so I'll page it and
  fetch more as you scroll.

⏺ Now measuring the actual screen again.

  Called chrome-devtools 4 times, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The slow screen was Rules, not Rows — and it's fixed. Measured in the browser:

  ┌─────────────────────────────┬────────────────┬─────────────────────────┐
  │                             │     before     │          after          │
  ├─────────────────────────────┼────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
  │ Rule with 2911 findings,    │ 39,192 DOM     │ 1,035                   │
  │ expanded                    │ nodes          │                         │
  ├─────────────────────────────┼────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
  │ Rows list, first paint      │ 2466 rows      │ 100, then 100 more per  │
  │                             │ fetched        │ scroll                  │
  └─────────────────────────────┴────────────────┴─────────────────────────┘

  The findings table now renders one <tbody> per row instead of one <tr> —
  that's what makes it work on an ambiguous rule, where a row is a <tr> plus the
  full-width finding beneath it and a ref can't go on the fragment holding
  both. A table may have any number of <tbody> elements, so each becomes the
  element the virtualiser measures.

  The rows list fetches a hundred at a time and appends as you scroll; the
  footer reads "200 of 7750 matching loaded". Verified live: three requests,
  offset=0 then offset=100, DOM going 336 → 454 nodes while the loaded count
  doubled.

  One thing I did not do, deliberately: the row-state derivation still builds
  the whole filtered set before cutting a window from it, because state comes
  from findings. The unfiltered endpoint answers in 155ms on the real export, so
  putting that derivation into SQL across a union of three sources — ordered
  stably enough to page against — would be a lot of machinery for no measured
  gain. The window saves the payload and the parse, which is the part that
  grows.

❯ start with next phase , new patient intake is straightforward. do you have    
  any questions ?                                                               

  Searched for 1 pattern, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Two, and they're both structural.

  1. Does a submitted intake create a patient record, and is that the table 1.4 
     deferred? Part C wants "a patient detail view: current record, their
     intakes", which reads like one patient table holding both the migrated
     legacy rows and the newly-intaken ones. 1.4 explicitly parked that design
     including whether Part B writes into the same tables. If it's one table, it
     wants designing once now rather than twice — and promotion from the rows
     screen then has somewhere to promote to, which closes Part A properly.
  2. Is the eligibility engine the same engine as the rules engine, or a 
     separate one that happens to share the word "rules"? Both are versioned and
     both must explain themselves, but they do opposite things: a Part A rule
     proposes a change and waits for a human; a Part B ruleset classifies a
     submission on the spot with no human in the loop, and the intake stores
     which ruleset judged it. My instinct is a separate table and a separate
     registry — reusing rule/rule_version would put two unrelated lifecycles in
     one place, and needs_review on an eligibility ruleset would mean nothing.
     But you built the rules engine's shape deliberately, so I'd rather hear it
     from you.

  Everything else I'll decide: the form steps, the state machine as a transition
  table that makes illegal moves unrepresentable rather than merely unused, the
  audit log as its own table, and capturing height and weight units explicitly
  on the form — Part A's weight_unit mess is the argument for never inferring
  them.

       85 +| `ruleset_version` | text | null until evaluated; null for le
          +gacy |                                                        
       86 +| `full_name`, `email`, `date_of_birth` | text | as `patient` 
          +|                                                             
       87 +| `height_cm`, `weight_kg` | real | stored rounded to 1 dp |  
       88 +| `height_entered`, `weight_entered` | text | simple-json `{va
          +lue, unit}` as typed |                                        
       89 +| `bmi` | real | 1 dp; null for legacy |                      
       90 +| `glp1_current` | integer | boolean; null for legacy |       
       91 +| `glp1_medications` | text | simple-json `string[]` |        
       92 +| `other_medications` | text | null; legacy `meds_current` lan
          +ds here |                                                     
       93 +| `weight_conditions` | text | simple-json `string[]` |       
       94 +| `thyroid_cancer_history` | integer | boolean; null for legac
          +y |                                                           
       95 +| `pancreatitis_history` | integer | boolean; null for legacy 
          +|                                                             
       96 +| `other_conditions` | text | null; legacy `conditions` lands 
          +here |                                                        
       97 +| `alcohol_units_week` | integer | null |                     
       98 +| `evaluation` | text | simple-json `[{ruleId, matched, outcom
          +e, explanation}]` |                                           
       99 +| `patient_id` | text | FK `patient.id`, null until approved |
      100 +| `legacy_intake_id` | text | null |                          
      101 +| `created_at`, `submitted_at`, `decided_at` | text | ISO UTC 
          +|                                                             
      102  
      103 +No note column: a decision's note is `audit_event.reason`.    
      104 +                                                              
      105 +### 2.1.3 `consent_event`                                     
      106 +                                                              
      107 +| Column | Type | Constraint |                                
      108 +|---|---|---|                                                 
      109 +| `id` | text | uuid, PK |                                    
      110 +| `patient_id` | text | FK, null |                            
      111 +| `intake_id` | text | FK, null; `CHECK` at least one of the t
          +wo |                                                          
      112 +| `type` | text | `CHECK IN ('data_processing')` |            
      113 +| `action` | text | `CHECK IN ('granted','revoked')` |        
      114 +| `version` | text | not null |                               
      115 +| `at` | text | ISO UTC, not null |                           
      116 +| `origin` | text | `CHECK IN (legacy, intake)` |             
      117 +| `legacy_id` | text | null |                                 
      118 +                                                              
      119 +Append-only, like the legacy consents it mirrors. Intake conse
          +nt carries                                                    
      120 +`intake_id`; approval sets `patient_id`.                      
      121 +                                                              
      122 +---                                                           
      123 +                                                              
      124 +## 2.2 Intake statuses                                        
      125 +                                                              
      126 +| From | To | Actor | Trigger |                               
      127 +|---|---|---|---|                                             
      128 +| — | `draft` | patient | consent given (2.3) |               
      129 +| `draft` | `submitted` | patient | submit |                  
      130 +| `submitted` | `auto_cleared` · `auto_flagged` · `auto_reject
          +ed` | `system` | evaluation, same transaction as submit |     
      131 +| `auto_cleared` · `auto_flagged` · `auto_rejected` | `in_revi
          +ew` | reviewer | start review |                               
      132 +| `in_review` | `approved` · `rejected` | reviewer | decide, n
          +ote required |                                                
      133 +| — | `approved` · `rejected` · `in_review` | `legacy-import` 
          +| legacy acceptance (2.6) |                                   
      134 +                                                              
      135 +Every other pair is illegal. `approved` and `rejected` are ter
          +minal. `in_review` =                                          
      136 +awaiting a human decision. `auto_rejected` can still be review
          +ed — a mistyped                                               
      137 +date of birth rejects wrongly.                                
      138 +                                                              
      139 +### Enforcement — impossible, not avoided                     
      140 +                                                              
      141 +1. `INTAKE_TRANSITIONS` constant: the only definition of legal
          + pairs.                                                       
      142 +2. `IntakeStateMachine.transition()` is the only write to `sta
          +tus`:                                                         
      143 +   `UPDATE … WHERE id = :id AND status = :from`, with its audi
          +t event, in one                                               
      144 +   transaction. 0 rows → 409.                                 
      145 +3. SQLite triggers, `CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS` on boot:   
      146 +   - `BEFORE UPDATE OF status` raises unless `(OLD.status, NEW
          +.status)` is legal.                                           
      147 +   - `BEFORE INSERT` raises unless `status = 'draft'`, or `ori
          +gin = 'legacy'` and                                           
      148 +     status is in the legacy set.                             
      149 +4. Tests attempt every illegal pair through the service and th
          +rough raw SQL; both fail.                                     
      150 +                                                              
      151 +---                                                           
      152 +                                                              
      153  ## 2.3 The intake form
      154  
       33 -Multi-step, moving through the statuses in 2.2.               
      155 +Multi-step, one questionnaire step per screen, moving through 
          +2.2.                                                          
      156  
       35 -The doc, "at minimum": identity (name, email, date of birth), 
          -body metrics                                                  
       36 -(height, weight), current medications, relevant conditions, an
          -d consent.                                                    
      157 +- Consent is step 1: nothing health-related is stored before c
          +onsent to process it.                                         
      158 +- Consent given → `POST /intakes` creates the `draft` and a `c
          +onsent_event granted`.                                        
      159 +- Each Next → `PATCH` saves that step; the server validates th
          +at step's fields.                                             
      160 +- Draft id in the URL and localStorage; resumable; read-only o
          +nce submitted.                                                
      161 +- No BMI and no likely outcome shown before submit — a live ve
          +rdict invites                                                 
      162 +  adjusting the weight until it passes.                       
      163 +- After submit the patient sees "received", never the outcome.
      164  
      165 +### 2.3.1 Questionnaire `q2026.1`                             
      166 +                                                              
      167 +Label distinct from legacy `v1`/`v2` so a stored label is neve
          +r mistaken for one.                                           
      168 +                                                              
      169 +**Step 1 — Consent**                                          
      170 +                                                              
      171 +| Field | Question | Input | Required |                       
      172 +|---|---|---|---|                                             
      173 +| `consent_data_processing` | I agree to Wellis processing my 
          +health data to assess my eligibility. (text `dp-2026.1`) | che
          +ckbox | must be checked |                                     
      174 +                                                              
      175 +**Step 2 — About you**                                        
      176 +                                                              
      177 +| Field | Question | Input | Required |                       
      178 +|---|---|---|---|                                             
      179 +| `full_name` | Full name | text | yes |                      
      180 +| `email` | Email address | email | yes |                     
      181 +| `date_of_birth` | Date of birth | date | yes |              
      182 +                                                              
      183 +**Step 3 — Body**                                             
      184 +                                                              
      185 +| Field | Question | Input | Required |                       
      186 +|---|---|---|---|                                             
      187 +| `height` | Height | number + unit `cm` · `ft + in` | yes |  
      188 +| `weight` | Weight | number + unit `kg` · `lb` | yes |       
      189 +                                                              
      190 +**Step 4 — Medication**                                       
      191 +                                                              
      192 +| Field | Question | Input | Required |                       
      193 +|---|---|---|---|                                             
      194 +| `glp1_current` | Are you currently using a GLP-1 medication,
          + such as Ozempic, Wegovy or Mounjaro? | yes / no | yes |      
      195 +| `glp1_medications` | Which? | multi-select: semaglutide (Oze
          +mpic, Wegovy, Rybelsus) · tirzepatide (Mounjaro, Zepbound) · l
          +iraglutide (Saxenda, Victoza) · dulaglutide (Trulicity) · exen
          +atide (Byetta, Bydureon) · other + text | when yes |          
      196 +| `other_medications` | Any other medication you currently use
          +? | long text | no |                                          
      197 +                                                              
      198 +**Step 5 — Health**                                           
      199 +                                                              
      200 +| Field | Question | Input | Required |                       
      201 +|---|---|---|---|                                             
      202 +| `weight_conditions` | Have you been diagnosed with any of th
          +ese? | multi-select: type 2 diabetes · prediabetes · high bloo
          +d pressure · high cholesterol · sleep apnoea · cardiovascular 
          +disease · none of these | yes |                               
      203 +| `thyroid_cancer_history` | Have you ever been diagnosed with
          + thyroid cancer? | yes / no | yes |                           
      204 +| `pancreatitis_history` | Have you ever had pancreatitis? | y
          +es / no | yes |                                               
      205 +| `other_conditions` | Any other conditions we should know abo
          +ut? | long text | no |                                        
      206 +| `alcohol_units_week` | How many units of alcohol do you drin
          +k in a typical week? | whole number | no |                    
      207 +                                                              
      208 +**Step 6 — Check and submit**: every answer by step, edit link
          + per step, Submit.                                            
      209 +                                                              
      210 +Yes/no questions have no default, and "none of these" is an ex
          +plicit choice: a                                              
      211 +deterministic rule must never read an unanswered question as "
          +no".                                                          
      212 +

       40 -For the intakes that need review.

       42 -The doc: "the submission, the rule evaluation with explanation
          -s, and                                                        
       43 -approve/reject with a required note."                         
      219 +- Default filter: `auto_flagged`, `auto_cleared`, `in_review`;
          + `auto_rejected` selectable.                                  
      220 +- Columns: submitted, age, BMI, status, matched flags. Oldest 
          +first. Virtualised.                                           
      221  
       45 -## 2.5 Form validation                                        
      222 +**Detail** — `GET /review/intakes/:id`                        
      223  
       47 -The form accepts values only in the correct format, checked in
          - the frontend.                                                
      224 +- Answers grouped by questionnaire step.                      
      225 +- Evaluation: every rule, matched or not, its explanation, the
          + ruleset version.

      232 +| Start review | `auto_*` | → `in_review`, actor recorded |   
      233 +| Approve | `in_review` | → `approved`; patient create-or-link
          + by email; `consent_event.patient_id` set |                   
      234 +| Reject | `in_review` | → `rejected`; no patient |           
      235 +                                                              
      236 +- Approve and Reject disabled until the note is non-empty afte
          +r trimming.

          + back and is shown                                            
      238 +  as in 2.6.                                                  
      239 +- Existing `patient` with the same email → linked, not duplica
          +ted. Else created,                                            
      240 +  `origin intake`, `account_status prospect`.                 
      241 +- Reviewer is a name entered once, kept in localStorage, sent 
          +as `actor`.

      244 +                                                              
      245 +## 2.5 Validation                                             
      246 +                                                              
      247 +- **Validation rejects impossible values; eligibility judges p
          +ossible ones.** Age                                           
      248 +  17 or BMI 22 passes validation and reaches `auto_rejected` w
          +ith an explanation.                                           
      249 +- Frontend: `@mantine/form` per step; errors on blur and on Ne
          +xt; Next blocked                                              
      250 +  until the step is valid.                                    
      251 +- Backend is authoritative: re-validates on every `PATCH` (tha
          +t step) and on                                                
      252 +  submit (all steps). 422 on failure.                         
      253 +- Same rules gate legacy acceptance (2.6) and patient creation
          + on approval (2.4).                                           
      254 +- Written twice — no shared package (tech-stack 4.10). A contr
          +act spec posts each                                           
      255 +  invalid fixture and asserts 422 naming that field.          
      256 +                                                              
      257 +| Field | Rule |                                              
      258 +|---|---|                                                     
      259 +| `full_name` | trim; 2–120 chars; ≥1 letter; no digit; no `@`
          + |                                                            
      260 +| `email` | trim, lowercase; ≤254; exactly one `@`; domain con
          +tains `.`; no whitespace |                                    
      261 +| `date_of_birth` | real calendar date; not after today; not b
          +efore 1900-01-01 |                                            
      262 +| `height` | 100–250 cm after conversion; `ft` 3–8, `in` 0–11 
          +|                                                             
      263 +| `weight` | 30–400 kg after conversion |                     
      264 +| `glp1_medications` | ≥1 when `glp1_current` yes; empty when 
          +no |                                                          
      265 +| `weight_conditions` | ≥1; "none of these" combines with noth
          +ing |                                                         
      266 +| yes / no fields | answered |                                
      267 +| `alcohol_units_week` | integer 0–200 |                      
      268 +| `consent_data_processing` | checked |                       
      269 +| `sex` (legacy) | `M` or `F` |                               
      270 +| `bsn` (legacy) | 9 digits; passes the eleven-proef |        
      271 +| `phone` (legacy) | E.164, `+` then 8–15 digits |            
      272 +| `account_status` (legacy) | `active` · `paused` · `churned` 
          +· `prospect` |                                                
      273 +| `signup_date` (legacy) | real date; not after today |       
      274 +                                                              
      275 +Conversions: `lb × 0.45359237`; `(ft × 12 + in) × 2.54`; round
          +ed half-up to 1 dp.                                           
      276 +BMI is computed from the stored rounded values, so they reprod
          +uce it.                                                       
      277 +                                                              
      278 +---                                                           
      279 +                                                              
      280  ## 2.6 Accepting an import row into the final table
      281  
       51 -When a row is accepted from its import status into the final t
          -able (2.1) and                                                
       52 -its data is not valid:                                        
      282 +`POST /rows/:table/:legacyId/accept`                          
      283  
       54 -- the error is caught                                         
       55 -- the user is shown the error                                 
       56 -- nothing in the database changes                             
      284 +### Preconditions — 409 with the reason                       
      285  
      286 +- Row state is Import clean: no pending finding, not rejected,
          + not accepted.                                                
      287 +- The legacy id names exactly one data row (1.0.3).           
      288 +- Not the duplicate side of a `duplicate` link (1.1.13).      
      289 +                                                              
      290 +### Mapping                                                   
      291 +                                                              
      292 +| Source | Target | Requires |                                
      293 +|---|---|---|                                                 
      294 +| patient | `patient`, `origin legacy`, `account_status` ← leg
          +acy `status` | every column passes 2.5; `weight_unit` is `kg` 
          +|                                                             
      295 +| intake | `intake`, `origin legacy`, status ← outcome: `appro
          +ved`→`approved`, `rejected`→`rejected`, `pending`→`in_review` 
          +| its patient already accepted; outcome is one of the three | 
      296 +| consent | `consent_event`, `origin legacy` | its patient alr
          +eady accepted; action is `granted` or `revoked`; `at` is ISO |
      297 +                                                              
      298 +Legacy free text is never parsed into answers: `glp1_current`,
          + `thyroid_cancer_history`,                                    
      299 +`pancreatitis_history` stay null (I26, I29). `reviewer_note` →
          + the acceptance audit                                         
      300 +event's `reason`.                                             
      301 +                                                              
      302 +### Transaction                                               
      303 +                                                              
      304 +Validate every column → insert target → insert `row_acceptance
          +` → `audit_event`                                             
      305 +(`legacy_row`, `accept`). Any failure → rollback, nothing writ
          +ten.                                                          
      306 +                                                              
      307 +### Errors — the user's requirement                           
      308 +                                                              
      309 +- Invalid data → 422 `{ message, errors: [{ field, value, reas
          +on }] }`.                                                     
      310 +- Every field error collected in one pass, not the first only.
      311 +- Database constraint errors (unique email) caught and returne
          +d as a field error.                                           
      312 +- Nothing leaks as a 500.                                     
      313 +- UI: Accept on Import clean rows; on 422 an alert lists each 
          +field, its value                                              
      314 +  (drawn with `ValueDiff`, so invisible characters show) and t
          +he reason; the row                                            
      315 +  stays Import clean.                                         
      316 +                                                              
      317 +### `row_acceptance`                                          
      318 +                                                              
      319 +| Column | Type | Constraint |                                
      320 +|---|---|---|                                                 
      321 +| `source_table` | text | PK |                                
      322 +| `legacy_id` | text | PK |                                   
      323 +| `target_table` | text | `CHECK IN (patient, intake, consent_
          +event)` |                                                     
      324 +| `target_id` | text | not null |                             
      325 +| `accepted_at` | text | ISO UTC |                            
      326 +                                                              
      327 +Rows screen gains a fourth state, **Imported**. Precedence: im
          +ported > rejected >                                           
      328 +pending > clean. Final: no actions, skipped by Apply rules.   
      329 +                                                              
      330  ---
      331  
       60 -## Open questions                                             
      332 +## 2.7 Eligibility engine                                     
      333  
       62 -- **2.a** Is the eligibility engine the Part A rules engine, o
          -r a separate one?                                             
       63 -  Both are versioned and explain themselves, but a Part A rule
          - proposes a change                                            
       64 -  and waits for a human, while an eligibility ruleset classifi
          -es a submission on                                            
       65 -  the spot and the intake records which ruleset judged it.    
       66 -- **2.b** The doc also requires a deterministic eligibility ru
          -les engine (its                                               
       67 -  six-row rules table) and an audit log of every state change 
          -and human decision.                                           
       68 -  Neither is a node yet.                                      
      334 +Separate from the Part A rules engine: a Part A rule proposes 
          +a change and waits                                            
      335 +for a human; a ruleset classifies a submission on the spot. Re
          +solves 2.a.                                                   
      336 +                                                              
      337 +- Rulesets are code, registered by version; every version stay
          +s registered.                                                 
      338 +- `ACTIVE_RULESET = 'elig-1'`. Changing a threshold is a new v
          +ersion.                                                       
      339 +- Evaluated once, at submit; the intake stores `ruleset_versio
          +n` and every rule's result.                                   
      340 +- Never re-evaluated.                                         
      341 +                                                              
      342 +### `elig-1`                                                  
      343 +                                                              
      344 +| Rule | Condition | Outcome | Explanation |                  
      345 +|---|---|---|---|                                             
      346 +| E1 | age < 18 | reject | `rejected: age 17 at submission, un
          +der 18` |                                                     
      347 +| E2 | BMI < 27.0 | reject | `rejected: BMI 25.3, below 27` | 
      348 +| E3 | 27.0 ≤ BMI ≤ 30.0 and `weight_conditions` = none | flag
          + | `flagged: BMI 27.4 with no weight-related condition` |     
      349 +| E4 | `glp1_current` yes | flag | `flagged: currently using a
          + GLP-1 medication (semaglutide)` |                            
      350 +| E5 | thyroid cancer or pancreatitis history | flag | `flagge
          +d: self-reported history of pancreatitis` |                   
      351 +| — | nothing matched | clear | `cleared: no rule matched, for
          + doctor review` |                                             
      352 +                                                              
      353 +- Outcome: any reject → `auto_rejected`; else any flag → `auto
          +_flagged`; else `auto_cleared`.                               
      354 +- All five always run; every result stored, so a reviewer sees
          + every reason.                                                
      355 +- Age: whole years from `date_of_birth` to the `submitted_at` 
          +date, UTC.                                                    
      356 +- BMI: `weight_kg / (height_cm / 100)²`, rounded half-up to 1 
          +dp, **compared                                                
      357 +  rounded** — otherwise 26.96 shows "27.0" and rejects as belo
          +w 27.                                                         
      358 +                                                              
      359 +---                                                           
      360 +                                                              
      361 +## 2.8 Audit log                                              
      362 +                                                              
      363 +Every state change and every human decision. Resolves 2.b.    
      364 +                                                              
      365 +| Column | Type | Constraint |                                
      366 +|---|---|---|                                                 
      367 +| `id` | text | uuid, PK |                                    
      368 +| `entity` | text | `CHECK IN (intake, patient, consent_event,
          + legacy_row)` |                                               
      369 +| `entity_id` | text | not null |                             
      370 +| `action` | text | `CHECK IN (create, transition, decision, a
          +ccept)` |                                                     
      371 +| `from_state` | text | null |                                
      372 +| `to_state` | text | null |                                  
      373 +| `actor` | text | `system`, `legacy-import`, or a reviewer na
          +me |                                                          
      374 +| `reason` | text | null; required for `decision` |           
      375 +| `at` | text | ISO UTC |                                     
      376 +                                                              
      377 +- Written in the transaction of the change it records.        
      378 +- Append-only: triggers raise on `UPDATE` and `DELETE`.       
      379 +- Part A's rule rows stay the log of legacy value changes (1.3
          +); the patient                                                
      380 +  history (Part C) reads both.                                
      381 +                                                              
      382 +---                                                           
      383 +                                                              
      384 +## 2.9 API                                                    
      385 +                                                              
      386 +| Method | Path | Does | Errors |                             
      387 +|---|---|---|---|                                             
      388 +| POST | `/intakes` | consent → `draft` | 422 |               
      389 +| PATCH | `/intakes/:id` | save one step | 409 not draft · 422
          + |                                                            
      390 +| POST | `/intakes/:id/submit` | validate all, `submitted` → `
          +auto_*` | 409 · 422 |                                         
      391 +| GET | `/intakes/:id` | patient view, neutral status | 404 | 
      392 +| GET | `/review/intakes` | queue, status filter | 400 |      
      393 +| GET | `/review/intakes/:id` | answers, evaluation, history |
          + 404 |                                                        
      394 +| POST | `/review/intakes/:id/start` | `auto_*` → `in_review` 
          +| 409 |                                                       
      395 +| POST | `/review/intakes/:id/decide` | `{ decision, note, act
          +or }` | 409 · 422 |                                           
      396 +| POST | `/rows/:table/:legacyId/accept` | 2.6 | 409 · 422 |  
      397 +                                                              
      398 +422 body everywhere: `{ message, errors: [{ field, value, reas
          +on }] }`.                                                     
      399 +                                                              
      400 +---                                                           
      401 +                                                              
      402 +## Limits                                                     
      403 +                                                              
      404 +- No authentication: a draft is reached by uuid; `actor` is a 
          +typed name.                                                   
      405 +- A ruleset change does not re-evaluate past intakes.         
      406 +- Legacy weights in lbs cannot be accepted until a pounds→kg r
          +ule exists                                                    
      407 +  (`rule-catalogue.md`, its unsatisfiable constraint).        
      408 +- The duplicate side of a link is refused, never merged (defer
          +red D5).                                                      
      409 +- A returning patient with a new email becomes a new patient —
          + the ops team's                                               
      410 +  retry case stays a duplicates problem.                      
      411 +- Work queue across Part A and B, conflict view, patient detai
          +l: Part C.                                                    
      412 +                                                              
      413 +---                                                           
      414 +                                                              
      415 +## Tasks                                                      
      416 +                                                              
      417 +| Id | Task | Nodes |                                         
      418 +|---|---|---|                                                 
      419 +| B1 | Entities `patient`, `intake`, `consent_event`, `row_acc
          +eptance`, `audit_event`; boot triggers | 2.1, 2.2, 2.6, 2.8 | 
      420 +| B2 | Backend validators and invalid fixtures | 2.5 |        
      421 +| B3 | Intake state machine and audit writer | 2.2, 2.8 |     
      422 +| B4 | Eligibility engine `elig-1` | 2.7 |                    
      423 +| B5 | Intake API: create, patch, submit | 2.3, 2.9 |         
      424 +| B6 | Acceptance service, endpoint, rows screen Imported stat
          +e | 2.6 |                                                     
      425 +| B7 | Review API: queue, detail, start, decide with patient c
          +reate-or-link | 2.4 |                                         
      426 +| B8 | Intake form UI with `@mantine/form` | 2.3, 2.5 |       
      427 +| B9 | Review UI | 2.4 |                                      
      428 +| B10 | Accept button and error display on the rows screen | 2
          +.6 |

⏺ Update(notes/tech-stack.md)
  ⎿  Added 5 lines, removed 1 line
      10  
      11  ### 4.1 Frontend
      12  - React + TypeScript, built with Vite.
      13 -- Mantine as the component library, core packages only.        
      13 +- Mantine as the component library: `@mantine/core`, plus `@man
         +tine/form` for the                                             
      14 +  multi-step intake form's state and per-step validation (intak
         +e 2.5).                                                        
      15  - No data grid library. Mantine's `Table` is a styling primitiv
          e, not a grid, and
      16    our tables are simple. Revisit only when a real need appears.
      17 +- `@tanstack/react-virtual` for long lists. Mantine core has no
         + virtualiser; it                                               
      18 +  is headless, so it draws into Mantine's own `Table` and `Acco
         +rdion` markup                                                  
      19 +  rather than replacing them.                                  
      20  - Routing: `react-router-dom`. Plain nested routes, no data loa

⏺ The Part B spec is written into notes/intake-requirements.md. It covers the
  architecture, the final tables, the eight statuses, the questionnaire
  (q2026.1), validation, accepting import rows, the eligibility engine, the
  audit log, the API, and tasks B1–B10. I also added @mantine/form to the
  tech-stack note, and wrote down @tanstack/react-virtual, which I'd added
  earlier without recording it. Both files are committed and pushed.

  I settled both open questions and made a few other calls on your behalf. These
  four are the ones worth checking before anything gets built:

  1. A new intake becomes a patient only when it's approved. Until then it sits
     in the intake table on its own. That means a rejected applicant never lands
     in the patient table. It also means import rows and intakes both enter it
     through an explicit accept, with the same validation.
  2. The consent question is step 1, not the last step. Otherwise the form would
     save health answers before the patient has agreed to their processing.
     This changes the order of the assignment's minimum field list.
  3. An automatically rejected intake can still be opened for review. The
     assignment's arrow sends all three automatic outcomes on to review. It's
     also useful: someone who mistypes their date of birth gets rejected wrongly
     and needs a way back.
  4. Import rows weighed in pounds can't be accepted yet. No pounds→kg rule
     exists; the rule catalogue already notes it can't be written as things
     stand. I've listed this under Limits rather than working around it.
     [DECISION] Leave it as a known gap, or build the two-column fix it needs
     before accepting import rows.

  The eligibility engine is separate from the rules engine, as I suggested
  before. The file says why.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ No real reason once the intake is validated, so I merged it: the spec now has
  the legacy tables and one main patient table. I'd split them for two reasons:
  so a rejected applicant wouldn't count as a patient, and so one person could
  send in several intakes over time. Merging drops the second and changes two
  things:

  1. One row per email. A second intake with an email already in the table is
     refused at step 1.
  2. Legacy intakes don't go into the main table. There are about 3,100 of them
     for 2,466 patients, and the main table holds one row per person. They stay
     in the legacy intake table as history, so on the rows screen only patient
     and consent rows get an Accept button.

  I also renamed the review status column to intake_status, so it doesn't clash
  with the commercial account_status that legacy patients already have.

  Your other answers are in and pushed. Consent is now the last form step.
  Height and weight are asked in cm and kg only. A legacy weight in pounds just
  fails acceptance, as intended, so I took that off the limits list.
  Auto-rejected intakes stay reviewable.

  Auto accept and auto reject for legacy data is open question 2.c, with task
  B11 waiting on it. What should decide whether a legacy row is accepted or
  rejected automatically, and which intake status should a legacy patient have
  once it's in the main table? The difficulty is that our eligibility check
  can't judge legacy patients fairly. Three of its five rules need yes/no
  answers (GLP-1 use, thyroid cancer, pancreatitis) that the legacy export only
  has as free text. Meanwhile each legacy intake already carries the old
  system's decision: approved, rejected or pending.

✻ Worked for 3m 20s · done 4:01 PM

※ recap: The Part B intake spec is written, using one main patient table plus the legacy tables. Next is your call on question 2.c: what should decide auto accept or auto reject for legacy rows, and what status an imported legacy patient gets. (disable recaps in /config)

❯ we did not build the duplicate logic ?                                        

  Searched for 2 patterns, ran 4 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ It's half built. The duplicate rules find duplicates, but nothing stores what
  they find, so the duplicates table is empty and no screen shows any.

  1. What exists:
     - The duplicates table.
     - A way for rules to report a link ("X is a duplicate of Y") instead of a
       column change.
     - All six duplicate rules, which return those links: same email, same
       citizen number (BSN), same name and date of birth, same phone, repeated
       intake id, identical consent event.
  2. What was never built:
     - Saving the links. "Apply rules" runs the six rules and throws their
       results away; the code that saves findings has no step for links. We
       missed it: the rule tasks built the rules, and none built the saving.
     - A screen for duplicates. Nothing lists them or lets you confirm or
       dismiss one.
     - Merging. Requirement 1.1.13 says a merge is an ordinary proposed change
       on Y, tagged as coming from a duplicate. No rule proposes one yet.
     - Retiring X. This one was deliberately put off (D5 in the deferred list).
  3. Why this matters for Part B: the import-accept spec I just wrote refuses to
     accept a row marked as a duplicate. With the table empty, that check never
     blocks anything.
     - A same-email pair isn't fully caught: the second one hits the
       one-row-per-email rule and fails with an error.
     - People who signed up twice with different emails would both go into the
       main table without any warning. That's exactly the case the ops team's
       notes describe.

  [DECISION] Build saving the links and a duplicates screen before accepting
  import rows, or accept rows first and live with duplicates getting through
  until it exists.

⏺ Workflow(duplicates.js)
  ⎿  /workflows to view dynamic workflow runs

⏺ Duplicate handling is written up as 1.7 in final-requirements.md, and a
  workflow is now building it: five tasks, one after another. Here's what it
  covers:

  1. Saving the links. "Apply rules" will keep every duplicate link the rules
     find. A pair gets one link only, and a link you dismiss won't come back on
     later runs.
  2. Fixing the intake and consent duplicate rules. All six rules get a new
     version that names the two actual rows, not just their legacy ids. The old
     versions stay registered but switched off.
  3. Confirm and dismiss, with a screen. It lists links, shows both rows side by
     side with the differences highlighted, and has Confirm and Dismiss
     buttons.
  4. Retiring X. Confirming a patient link marks X as Import rejected, with the
     reason naming Y. You can undo that like any other rejection. This was
     deferred item D5, and it can be built now because rows have a rejected
     state. For intakes and consents both rows share one id, so nothing gets
     rejected. Accepting import rows instead skips the confirmed duplicate.
  5. Merging. Seven new rules, one per column, fill an empty column on Y with
     X's value. They only cover facts about the person: name, email, date of
     birth, sex, citizen number, phone and city. Signup date, source, status,
     weight and height stay as they are, since they describe a particular signup
     rather than the person.

⏺ Both remaining features are set up: duplicates is still building, and Part B
  starts as soon as it finishes.

  1. Duplicates: saving links and the new versions of the six rules are done.
     Confirm/dismiss, the seven merge rules and the duplicates screen are still
     being built.
  2. Part B is written into the spec and has its own workflow ready, seven tasks
     one after another:
     - the main table, the status rules and the audit log
     - the eligibility check
     - the intake and review API
     - importing legacy patients
     - the intake form
     - the review screen
     - importing from the rows screen

     It has to wait. If both workflows ran at once, each one's test runs would
     hit the other's half-written code.
  3. Legacy import follows your rule. Importing a clean legacy patient creates a
     new patient entry in the main table. It goes through the same eligibility
     check and lands in the same review queue as an intake.
     - Legacy rows only have free text for GLP-1 use, thyroid cancer and
       pancreatitis, so they can't answer those three questions. A missing
       answer counts as flagged, never cleared. A legacy import therefore comes
       out flagged or rejected, never cleared, and always ends up with a
       reviewer.
     - The rows screen gets an Import button per clean patient row, plus
       checkboxes, select-all and Import selected.
     - Bulk import handles each row separately, so one bad row doesn't stop the
       rest. Failed rows stay selected, with each field error listed.
  4. Not in either build: changing two columns as one fix (1.1.4). No rule
     proposes two columns at once. The only example was converting pounds to kg,
     and your rule that legacy weights must already be in kg removes the need
     for it. [DECISION] Leave it out, or build it after Part B.

⏺ I have two open questions. Everything else in the spec is settled.

  1. [DECISION] What happens when a returning patient starts a new intake? The
     main table allows only one row per email. So if a legacy patient has been
     imported and later fills in the intake form with the same email, they're
     stopped at step 1 with "email taken" and can't go further. Should the form
     pick up their existing record, open a new intake for that same patient, or
     send them somewhere else?
  2. [DECISION] Fixing two columns together (1.1.4). I'm still waiting on your
     call. Nothing needs it today: pounds to kg was the only example, and your
     rule that legacy weights must already be in kg removes it. Do you want it
     built at all, and if so, before or after Part B?

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ No, duplicates isn't batched, and that's why it's slow. Part B is now set up
  to build in batches.

  1. Duplicates builds its five pieces one after another. Each piece goes
     through four agents: plan, build, review, commit. Every agent reads the
     code again and runs the full build and tests, so each piece takes 12 to 30
     minutes. It's on the last piece, the duplicates screen, about an hour and a
     half in. I'm leaving it running rather than restarting.
  2. Part B now builds in three batches instead of seven separate tasks:
     - the main table plus the eligibility check
     - the intake API plus legacy import
     - the three screens

     That's 9 agents instead of 21. They still run one after another so their
     test runs don't collide. It starts when duplicates finishes.
  3. Email check: an email that already belongs to a submitted or imported
     patient is now caught on submit, not at step 1. The form sends the person
     back to step 1 with the error, and nothing is saved. Unfinished drafts can
     share an email, so an abandoned draft no longer blocks anyone. The spec is
     updated.
  4. Fixing two columns together (1.1.4) stays open. It's not in either build.

⏺ Workflow(intake.js)
  ⎿  /workflows to view dynamic workflow runs

⏺ The intake plan is clear enough that the build is running now, but two
  questions about importing legacy patients need your answer before the import
  part is built.

  1. Duplicates is finished. All five pieces are committed, the duplicates
     screen included.
  2. The Part B build has started. It runs in three batches, and the import work
     is in the second one. The first batch builds the main table, the status
     rules and the eligibility check.
  3. One gap I closed myself: a legacy patient can't be imported while a
     duplicate link on them is still pending. If they could, confirming the link
     afterwards would try to merge values into a record that no longer takes
     changes, and the merge would be quietly lost.
  4. [DECISION] What should happen to a legacy patient with no height or weight?
     The spec contradicts itself here:
     - The validation section says import requires height and weight, so the
       import would fail with an error.
     - The eligibility section says a missing answer is flagged, so the patient
       would come in flagged "BMI not recorded" for a reviewer.
  5. [DECISION] What should happen to a legacy patient whose data consent is 
     revoked, or who has no consent record? An intake can't be submitted without
     consent. The import currently copies their consent history but doesn't
     check it, so they would reach the review queue anyway.

✻ Cooked for 1m 27s · done 6:22 PM

❯ 3-> avoid approving if its a duplicate and not resolved ?????                 

⏺ Yes. A legacy patient who is part of an unresolved duplicate can't be imported
  until someone confirms or dismisses that duplicate on the duplicates screen.

  1. The copy was already blocked. The duplicate finding sits on it, so it never
     counted as clean.
  2. The original wasn't blocked, and now is. It carried no finding, so it
     showed as clean and could be imported while the duplicate was still
     unresolved. Confirming the duplicate afterwards would have tried to copy
     missing values from the copy into a record that no longer takes changes.
     Those values would have been lost.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Point 4 is in the spec: a legacy patient with no height or weight is imported
  and rejected automatically, with the reason "BMI not recorded". A reviewer can
  still open them.

  On point 5, here's what I meant. The legacy data includes each patient's
  consent history: when they agreed to have their data processed, and in some
  cases when they withdrew it. A new patient can't submit an intake without
  ticking the consent box, so every intake patient has agreed. A legacy patient
  might not have. Their last consent entry could be a withdrawal, or they might
  have no consent entry at all. As the import is built today, those patients are
  imported and reviewed like everyone else.

  [DECISION] Should a legacy patient who withdrew consent, or never gave it, be
  imported at all?
