⏺ SendFeedback(Bulk-select UI built as a non-standard layout instead of row
              checkboxes + header Select all)

⏺ The rows screen now uses the standard pattern: a checkbox before each clean
  row, and Select all in the list header, lined up in the same column. I checked
  the alignment in the browser; it's pushed.

  1. Only on the Patient tab. The checkboxes, Select all and the Import buttons
     appear only there, and only while the Import clean rows are in view.
     They're gone from Intake, Consent and All.
  2. Why other rows got imported. The old select-all showed on every tab and
     selected clean patient rows even when the list wasn't showing them. Now it
     only selects clean rows in the current list, including ones you haven't
     scrolled to yet. Changing a filter clears the selection.
  3. Name field removed. Imports record the name saved on the review screen, or
     "staff" if none has been entered.

✻ Worked for 4m 37s · done 9:01 PM

※ recap: Part B (patient intake, review queue and legacy import) is finished and pushed, and the rows screen now uses row checkboxes with an aligned Select all. Next is your call between the Part C review console, deployment, and AGENT-NOTES.md.

❯ [Image #1]                                                                    
                                                                                
  you have two menus ,                                                          
  what is the meaning of them.                                                  
  ⎿  [Image #1]
⏺ They're two filters that work together. The first chooses which legacy table
  the rows come from, and the second chooses what state the rows are in.

  1. Table: Patient, Intake or Consent, the three tables in the legacy export.
     All shows every table.
  2. State:
     - Import pending: at least one rule finding on the row is still waiting for
       approve or decline.
     - Import clean: nothing pending and not rejected, so the row is ready to
       import.
     - Import rejected: someone rejected the row, or it was confirmed as a
       duplicate. It will never be imported.
     - Imported: the patient is already in the main patient table.

     Combined, Patient + Import clean is the list of patients you can import
     now.
  3. One mismatch: only patient rows are ever imported. Intake rows stay as
     history, and consent records come in with their patient. So on the Intake
     and Consent tabs, "Import clean" only means nothing is pending, and
     "Imported" never appears there.

  Called chrome-devtools 2 times, ran 1 shell command, wrote 1 memory (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ I fixed all three problems, checked each in the browser, and pushed.

  1. Filters. There's no "All" tab any more; you pick one table at a time:
     Patient, Intake or Consent.
     - Each tab says under the heading what its rows are.
     - Only the Patient tab uses the import states: Import pending, Import
       clean, Import rejected, Imported.
     - Intake and Consent use Needs review, Clean and Rejected, and have no
       Imported state.
  2. Height. The list sat in a fixed 640px box. That was an easy choice with the
     virtual-list library, not a reason. Lists now scroll with the page and use
     the full window height, on the Rows, Duplicates and Review screens. On the
     Patient tab, the Select all bar stays pinned to the top while you scroll,
     and the checkboxes still line up.
  3. Row details. An expanded row now reads top to bottom:
     - A status line, such as "3 findings waiting for a decision", with that
       row's actions beside it. Approve all and Decline all only appear when
       something is pending.
     - The values as a Field / Value table, with an Edit button on each field.
       It edits in place, with Save, Set to none and Cancel. This replaces the
       separate edit form.
     - Findings grouped by rule, shown only when there are findings. The Source
       and Legacy id columns are gone, since every finding belongs to that row.
     - Decline all and Reject row ask for confirmation in a dialog, with an
       optional reason, instead of the always-open reason fields.
     - Rejected and imported rows are read-only.

✻ Sautéed for 1m 8s · done 9:17 PM

❯ why is a row , which still has a rule , is in "import clean" , what is the    
  logic behind htat                                                             

  Searched for 2 patterns, ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ No clean row has a rule still waiting. The rules you see on a clean row have
  already been decided, and the panel keeps showing them, which makes them look
  open.

  1. The logic: a patient is Import clean when nothing on it is still pending,
     it isn't rejected, and it isn't imported. I checked all 131 clean patients:
     none has a pending finding. 103 have only findings that were already
     approved or declined, and 28 never had any.
     - Approved: the value was changed to the rule's proposal.
     - Declined: someone chose to keep the original value, so the rule never
       raises it again.
  2. Why it looks wrong: the row panel still lists those decided findings under
     Findings, each with its rule name as a heading. The only sign they're
     finished is a small grey "Settled" label. It also doesn't say whether each
     was approved or declined, because the backend returns both together as
     "settled".

  [DECISION] Should decided findings move out of Findings into a collapsed
  history section, marked Approved or Declined? That needs the backend to return
  the status separately, which is a small change.

⏺ The intake form was built, but nothing in the app linked to it, so you could
  only open it by typing /intake. There's now a "New patient intake" button on
  the right of the staff nav that opens it in a new tab. The change is pushed.

  1. The form: six steps, in order: About you, Body, Medication, Health,
     Consent, and Check and submit. Each step checks its own fields before you
     can go on. Drafts save as you go and can be picked up later. After
     submitting, the patient sees only "received", and the intake lands in the
     Review queue. I opened it in the browser and step 1 loads; I haven't
     submitted a test intake end to end.
  2. Row panel: with the API back up, I checked a clean row. It says "No open
     problems", then shows the values and a Log with Accepted entries and their
     before and after values.

  Ran 4 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ No. I found no such workflow in the notes, the saved memory or the earlier
  session transcripts, so I've written one. The nearest things already there are
  the revision workflow, which writes and activates a new rule version, and
  Apply rules, which records findings. Neither shows what a change does before
  it lands.

  1. What it shows: both versions of the rule run against the current data and
     nothing is saved. For the new version compared with the old, it reports:
     - what it newly finds, stops finding, or proposes differently
     - how many new findings Apply rules would record
     - how many Import clean rows would become pending, and so stop being
       importable
     - which findings are skipped because someone already declined them or the
       patient is already imported
     - which old-version findings stay pending and still need a decision
  2. The pieces:
     - The backend call is GET /rules/<ruleId>/effects. By default it compares
       the newest version with the one before.
     - The new rule-effects workflow uses a single agent. Give it rule ids, or
       none to take the rules changed in the last commit. It calls that
       endpoint, then summarises the change with examples and anything a
       reviewer should check before pressing Apply rules. It never builds,
       activates a version, or applies anything, so it can't knock over your
       running dev server.
     - The requirement is written up as 1.5.3.
  3. Checked: a new test covers every category above. All 1,059 tests pass, and
     it's pushed. On your running API, P47 (version 1 compared with version 2)
     shows no effects, because the 18 empty units were already set to kg. I
     haven't run the workflow itself yet.

✻ Cooked for 7m 2s · done 10:04 PM
